I just feel kind of gross or uncomfortable sometimes, but I haven’t done that.\n\nDoctor: After those nights when you eat a lot, do you ever take laxatives to try to undo it?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t taken laxatives for that. I don’t really do anything like that afterward.\n\nDoctor: Is your weight or body shape one of the main things that determines how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I don’t love feeling bloated or whatever after eating a lot, but my weight or shape isn’t really the main thing that decides how I feel about myself.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes happen after you wake up from sleep during the night?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. It’s more like I’ll be up late or wake up and then end up tearing through takeout or snacks after midnight. Other days I kind of forget to eat meals at all, so my eating’s just been all over the place.\n\nDoctor: When you eat after waking up at night, are you fully awake and aware of what you’re doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I’m awake. I know what I’m doing. It’s not like I’m sleepwalking or anything. My eating’s just been really all over the place—some days I barely remember to eat meals, and then other nights I’m up after midnight going through takeout or snacks.\n\nDoctor: In the morning, do you usually have little or no appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah, some mornings I don’t really feel like eating. It’s not every single day, but my appetite’s been pretty messed up overall—like I’ll skip meals without meaning to, then later at night I’m eating a bunch of takeout or snacks.\n\nDoctor: Do these nighttime eating episodes upset you or cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does bother me. Honestly, that’s one of the things that scared me enough to come in—my eating’s been all over the place. Some days I’ll forget meals completely, and then other nights I’m up after midnight tearing through takeout or snacks.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I’d say. It’s been going on for about four months now. I’ve been feeling low, guilty, and just not really caring about much, and it got harder to ignore after the trouble at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I used to actually enjoy doing stuff—playing soccer on weekends, cooking for my girlfriend, going to little galleries, even helping the newer guys at work. Lately I just… can’t really make myself care about any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost weight during this time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if I’ve actually lost weight. My eating’s just been really inconsistent—some days I don’t eat much or forget meals, and then other nights I’m up late eating a bunch of takeout or snacks.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts of wanting to die or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve had thoughts like I wish I could just not wake up for a while. But I haven’t made any plan to hurt myself.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up and eat during the night, is it usually because you couldn’t get back to sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, usually. A lot of nights I can’t fall asleep in the first place because my mind keeps running through work stuff, like pitches. Or I wake up way before my alarm and feel wired, even though I know I barely slept.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don’t think I’m scared of gaining weight—I just haven’t been eating on a normal schedule.\n\nDoctor: When you eat during the night, do you usually feel hungry?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah, but not always. My eating’s just been really all over the place. Some days I’ll forget to eat meals completely, and then other nights after midnight I’m going through takeout and snacks. That’s honestly one of the things that kind of scared me into coming in.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your mood gets worse in the evenings?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed a pattern like that. Some evenings are rough, but I couldn’t say it consistently gets worse at night.\n\nDoctor: During those nighttime eating episodes, do you usually eat an unusually large amount of food for the situation?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes it feels like a lot for that time of night. Like I’ll skip meals during the day, then after midnight I’m eating takeout and snacks and kind of just tearing through it. That’s part of what freaked me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you eat more than a quarter of your day’s food after your evening meal?\n\nPatient: Yeah, probably on some nights.
